<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that there are absolute constants <i>c</i> &gt; 0 and <i>C</i> &lt; ∞ such that every set $`A\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}`$ with no nonzero square difference satisfies $`|A|\le C N^{1-c}`$. This answers the fixed-power question posed by Green and Sawhney.

- **stmt-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that, for every integer $N\ge1$ and every square-difference-free set $A\subseteq[N]$, $$\|A\|\le C N^{1-c}.$$ Equivalently, every subset of $[N]$ larger than $C N^{1-c}$ contains distinct elements whose absolute difference is the square of a positive integer.
  - Explanation: A uniform absolute power saving bounds the cardinality of every square-difference-free subset of an integer interval.
- **stmt-lem-graph-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-count)): Let $p$ be an odd prime and let $G=(W,E)$ be a finite directed graph with no loops and at most one edge on each unordered pair of distinct vertices. Put $m=\|E\|$, and let $(z_w)_{w\in W}$ be independent uniform elements of $\mathbb F_p$. Then $$\left\| \mathbb E\prod_{(u,v)\in E}A_p(z_v-z_u)-2^{-m} \right\| \le m\delta_p.$$ The assertion includes the empty graph, whose product is $1$.
  - Explanation: Independent uniform field labels satisfy a graph constraint count with an error controlled by the number of edges. Loops and repeated unordered edges are excluded.
- **stmt-lem-list-mixing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-list-mixing)): Let $I,J$ be finite disjoint sets, and let $G$ be a directed graph on $I\sqcup J$ whose $m$ edges all join $I$ to $J$, with at most one edge on each unordered pair. The directions may vary from edge to edge. For $x\in\mathbb F_p^I$, $y\in\mathbb F_p^J$, write $z_i=x_i$ for $i\in I$, $z_j=y_j$ for $j\in J$, and set $$K(x,y)=\prod_{(u,v)\in E(G)}A_p(z_v-z_u).$$ Then $$\\|K-2^{-m}\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le (32m)^{1/4}p^{-1/8}. \label{eq:list-mixing}$$ For $m=0$, both sides are zero. In particular, the implied constant for any fixed pair of lists is independent of $p$.
  - Explanation: The square-constraint kernel between disjoint lists is close to a constant operator. Edge directions may vary, and the empty-edge case is included.
- **stmt-prop-tuple-laws** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-tuple-laws)): There is an absolute integer $P$ such that, for every prime $p\ge P$, there are a probability measure $\mu_p$ on $\mathbb F_p^V$ and a nonnegative submeasure $\varepsilon_p\le\mu_p$ whose mass satisfies $$\eta_p=\varepsilon_p(\mathbb F_p^V)\le p^{-4}.$$ Writing $$\mu_p^\circ=\frac{\mu_p-\varepsilon_p}{1-\eta_p},$$ the following properties hold.* 1. *The full law $\mu_p$ is reflection positive. Both $\mu_p$ and $\varepsilon_p$ are invariant under simultaneous changes $z_v\mapsto qz_v+a$, where $a\in\mathbb F_p$ and $q\in Q_p^*$. Every single-label marginal of $\mu_p$ and $\mu_p^\circ$ is uniform on $\mathbb F_p$.* 2. *On the support of $\mu_p^\circ$, $$z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}\in Q_p^*\qquad(0\le j<h).$$ The marginal of each ordered pair $(z_{v_j},z_{v_{j+1}})$ under $\mu_p^\circ$ is uniform on $\{(x,y):y-x\in Q_p^*\}$.* 3. *Put $\gamma=1/64$. For either $\rho_p=\mu_p$ or $\rho_p=\mu_p^\circ$, every $v\in V$, and every $f:\mathbb F_p\to\mathbb C$ with uniform mean zero, $$\left\\|\mathbb E_{\rho_p} \bigl[f(z_v)\mid(z_w)_{w\ne v}\bigr]\right\\|_2 \le p^{-\gamma}\\|f\\|_2. \label{eq:tuple-conditional-bound}$$ The norm on the left uses the other-label marginal of $\rho_p$; the norm on the right uses uniform measure on $\mathbb F_p$.
  - Explanation: The full finite-field law supplies reflection positivity and uniform marginals. Its normalized nonexceptional part supplies strict-square cycle support and exact pair marginals; both laws satisfy conditional mixing.
- **stmt-lem-tuple-component-symmetry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tuple-component-symmetry)): For every allowed mark set $S$ and every reflection $\theta$, the measure $\nu_{p,S}$ is invariant under the permutation $(z_v)\mapsto(z_{\theta v})$. Its matrix at that cut is symmetric.
  - Explanation: Reflection permutes each component measure without changing it, so the associated cut matrix is symmetric.
- **stmt-lem-marked-positive** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-positive)): Put $$\kappa=\frac9{10}\delta^{h-1},\qquad \lambda=(e^\kappa-1)^n,\qquad c_* =\lambda m_{t_0+1}^{-n}>0.$$ For every $S$ with $\|S\|\le t_0+1$, every cut in a marked block $b\in S$, and every real $F:\mathbb F_p^{V_+}\to\mathbb R$, $$Q_{p,S}^{b}(F) \ge \lambda m_s^{-n}\, \mathbb E_{Y,j_+}I_S(Y) F((Y_{u_S(v),j_v})_{v\in V_+})^2. \label{eq:tuple-marked-lower}$$ Here the expectation uses all uniform lists and only the uniform indices on $V_+$. In particular, the coefficient in <a href="#eq:tuple-marked-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tuple-marked-lower">[eq:tuple-marked-lower]</a> is at least $c_*$, independently of the prime and of the field-list values.
  - Explanation: A marked cut gives a strictly positive quadratic lower bound, uniformly over primes and field-list values.
- **stmt-lem-unmarked-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-unmarked-error)): Let $b\notin S$ specify a cut and suppose $s=\|S\|\le t_0$. Let $Y_+$ consist of the lists at plus addresses, and let $I_+(Y_+)$ impose only the constraints internal to those addresses. For a real half-label function $F$, put $$D_{p,S}^{b}(F)=\mathbb E_{Y_+,j_+}I_+(Y_+) F((Y_{u_S(v),j_v})_{v\in V_+})^2.$$ Then, with the fixed constant $C_*=(32d^2)^{1/4}B^2$, $$Q_{p,S}^{b}(F)\ge-C_*p^{-1/8}D_{p,S}^{b}(F). \label{eq:tuple-unmarked-error}$$ If instead $s=t_0+1$, the form $Q_{p,S}^{b}$ is nonnegative.
  - Explanation: List mixing controls the possible negative quadratic contribution at an unmarked cut. Terminal components have nonnegative cut forms.
- **stmt-lem-tuple-marking-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tuple-marking-domination)): Let $b\notin S$, $\|S\|\le t_0$, and set $S'=S\cup\{b\}$. For the cut in block $b$ and every real half-label function $F$, $$Q_{p,S'}^{b}(F)\ge c_*r^{-n}D_{p,S}^{b}(F). \label{eq:tuple-marking-domination}$$ Consequently the measure $\nu_{p,S}+p^{-1/16}\nu_{p,S'}$ has a positive semidefinite matrix at this cut whenever $$p\ge P_{\mathrm{ref}} :=\max\{3,(C_*r^n/c_*)^{16}\}. \label{eq:tuple-reflection-threshold}$$
  - Explanation: Marking another block dominates the unmarked-cut error; the weighted pair of measures is positive semidefinite beyond a fixed prime threshold.
- **stmt-lem-conditional-mixing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-mixing)): For the fixed index set $V$ and the measures constructed above, there is an absolute threshold $P$ such that the following statements hold for every prime $p\ge P$. Both probability measures $$\mu_p,\qquad \mu_p^\circ=\frac{\mu_p-\varepsilon_p}{1-\eta_p}, \qquad \eta_p=\varepsilon_p(1),$$ have uniform marginals in each individual label. If $\rho$ is either measure, $v\in V$, and $\lambda$ is its marginal on $\mathbb F_p^{V\setminus\{v\}}$, then every function $f:\mathbb F_p\to\mathbb C$ with $\mathbb E_x f(x)=0$ satisfies $$\left\\|\mathbb E_\rho\bigl[f(z_v)\mid(z_w)_{w\ne v}\bigr] \right\\|_{L^2(\lambda)} \le p^{-1/64}\\|f\\|_{L^2(\mathbb F_p)}. \label{eq:tuple-conditional-mixing}$$ The threshold is uniform in the choice of $v$ and $\rho$.* *For every selected cycle edge $v_j\to v_{j+1}$, its pair marginal under $\mu_p^\circ$ is uniform on $$\Omega_p=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb F_p^2:y-x\in Q_p^*\}.$$ Its pair marginal under $\mu_p$ is $$(1-\eta_p)\operatorname{Unif}(\Omega_p) +\eta_p\operatorname{Unif}(\mathbb F_p^2). \label{eq:tuple-full-pair-law}$$
  - Explanation: Conditioning on the other labels weakly predicts a centered single-label function. The nonexceptional cycle pair law is strict-square uniform; the full pair law also contains an exceptional independent pair.
- **stmt-prop-signed-holder** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-signed-holder)): For every finite prime set $\mathcal P$ as above and real functions $g,g_v:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb R$, one has $$Z_{\mathcal P}(g)\ge0, \qquad \left\|\mathcal Z_{\mathcal P}((g_v)_{v\in V})\right\| \le\prod_{v\in V}Z_{\mathcal P}(g_v)^{1/d}. \label{eq:signed-holder}$$ The inequality includes inputs with zero diagonal integral.
  - Explanation: Reflection positivity gives a mixed inequality for real signed inputs, including vanishing diagonal integrals.
- **stmt-lem-functional-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-functional-properties)): The map $g\mapsto\\|g\\|_{\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\mathcal P}$, and $$\|\mathbb E g\|\le\\|g\\|_{\mathcal P}. \label{eq:functional-mean}$$ For $a_p\in\mathbb F_p$ and $r_p\in Q_p^*$, let $A(x)_p=r_px_p+a_p$. Then $$\\|g\circ A\\|_{\mathcal P}=\\|g\\|_{\mathcal P}. \label{eq:functional-affine}$$ For every $B\subseteq\mathcal P$, $$\\|\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{\mathcal P\setminus B} =\\|\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{\mathcal P} \le\\|g\\|_{\mathcal P}. \label{eq:functional-projection}$$
  - Explanation: The diagonal functional defines a seminorm dominating the uniform mean, invariant under square-affine maps and contracting under uniform coordinate averaging.
- **stmt-lem-conductor-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conductor-decay)): Let $\mathcal P$ be a finite set of primes at least $P$, and at each prime choose $\rho_p$ to be either $\mu_p$ or $\mu_p^\circ$. Let $R\ge1$, and let $g_w:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb C$, $w\in V$, be functions. Suppose that for one slot $v$, $\widehat g_v(\xi)=0$ whenever $q(\xi)\le R$. Then $$\left\|\int\prod_{w\in V}g_w(z_w)\, d\!\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P}\rho_p(z)\right\| \le R^{-\gamma}\\|g_v\\|_2 \prod_{w\ne v}\\|g_w\\|_{2(d-1)}. \label{eq:conductor-decay}$$ All norms on the right use uniform probability measure on $X_{\mathcal P}$. The constant in the inequality is one.
  - Explanation: A slot with no low-conductor Fourier coefficients forces decay of the multilinear integral. The bound applies to either choice of prime law and complex inputs.
- **stmt-lem-lift-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lift-moments)): Let $Q\ge1$, let $I$ have length $L\ge10Q^6$, and suppose $\\|f\\|_\infty\le M$. Let $\mathcal F$ be any subfamily of $\{U\subseteq\mathcal P:q_U\le Q\}$, and put $$k=\max\{\|U\|:U\subseteq\mathcal P,\ q_U\le Q\}.$$ For every integer $r\ge1$, define $$K_{2r}=\left(\frac{(2r)!}{2^r r!}\right)^{1/(2r)}, \qquad A_r=2eK_{2r}\,2^{(r+3)/4}.$$ Then $$\left\\|\mathcal L^{\mathcal F}_{I,\mathcal P,Q}f\right\\|_{2r} \le 2M(k+1)A_r^k. \label{eq:lift-explicit-moment}$$ Consequently, for fixed $r$, this norm is at most $M Q^{o_r(1)}$ as $Q\to\infty$, uniformly in $I,f,\mathcal P$, and $\mathcal F$.
  - Explanation: Partial Fourier lifts have uniform fixed even-moment bounds when the interval is sufficiently long. Retention must use complete exact-denominator classes.
- **stmt-lem-lift-specialization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lift-specialization)): Let $B\subseteq\mathcal P$, let $z_B\in X_B$, and let $G:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb C$. For every finite $p\ge1$, $$\\|G(z_B,\cdot)\\|_{L^p(X_{\mathcal P\setminus B})} \le q_B^{1/p}\\|G\\|_{L^p(X_{\mathcal P})}. \label{eq:lift-specialization}$$ In the setting of Lemma <a href="#lem:lift-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lift-moments">15</a>, this gives, for every fixed $z_B$, $$\left\\|\bigl(\mathcal L^{\mathcal F}_{I,\mathcal P,Q}f\bigr) (z_B,\cdot)\right\\|_{2r} \le 2M q_B^{1/(2r)}(k+1)A_r^k.$$ The same bound holds for either part obtained by cutting the remaining denominator at any $H\ge1$: before specialization these parts correspond to the whole-support families $$\mathcal F_{\le H,B} =\{U\in\mathcal F:q_{U\setminus B}\le H\}, \qquad \mathcal F_{>H,B} =\{U\in\mathcal F:q_{U\setminus B}>H\}.$$ After specialization their Fourier supports lie, respectively, at remaining denominators at most $H$ and greater than $H$.
  - Explanation: Fixing prime coordinates costs an explicit fiber-size factor in a finite moment norm. Remaining-conductor truncations preserve their asserted frequency ranges.
- **stmt-prop-square-kernel** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-square-kernel)): Let $N\ge1$ be an integer, put $H=N^{\beta/2}$, and let $D=8d_0\le N^\beta$, where $d_0$ is odd and squarefree. Suppose $F,J:[N]\to\mathbb C$ satisfy $\|F\|,\|J\|\le1$ and are supported in residue classes $a,b$ modulo $D$, respectively. Assume that $b-a$ is a strict square class, that $x<y$ whenever $x\in\operatorname{supp}F$ and $y\in\operatorname{supp}J$, and that no such difference $y-x$ is the square of a positive integer. With $$\widehat F(\xi)=\frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N F(n)e(-n\xi), \qquad \widehat J(\xi)=\frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N J(n)e(-n\xi),$$ one has $$D\left\| \sum_{\substack{\xi\in\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z:\ q(\xi)\le H\\ q(\xi)\ \mathrm{squarefree},\ (q(\xi),D)=1}} \mathfrak g(\xi)\widehat F(-\xi)\widehat J(\xi) \right\| \ll H^{-1/3}. \label{eq:kernel-conclusion}$$ The implied constant is absolute and is independent of all the data in the statement.
  - Explanation: Ordered integer supports with strict-square residue differences and no actual positive square difference yield a small low-denominator quadratic Fourier sum.
- **stmt-lem-exact-fiber** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-fiber)): Let $N\ge1$, let $A\subseteq[N]$, and define $f_s$ by <a href="#eq:small-residue-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-residue-input">[eq:small-residue-input]</a>. Let $I\subseteq[N]$ be a nonempty interval, let $B\subseteq\mathcal P_N$, and put $$q_B=\prod_{p\in B}p,\qquad \mathcal S=\mathcal P_N\setminus B,\qquad D=M_0q_B.$$ Fix $s\bmod M_0$ and $z_B\in X_B$, and let $a\bmod D$ be their combined residue class. If $$g_I=(\mathcal L_{I,\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s)(z_B,\cdot),$$ then every character $\xi$ on $X_{\mathcal S}$ satisfying $q(\xi)q_B\le Q$ has coefficient $$\widehat g_I(\xi) =\frac D{\|I\|} \sum_{\substack{n\in I\\n\equiv a\pmod D}} 1_A(n)e(-n\xi). \label{eq:interval-fiber-coefficient}$$ Here $q_\varnothing=1$, and the hat denotes the normalized Fourier coefficient on the remaining product space.
  - Explanation: When the entire fixed-coordinate character group fits below the cutoff, specialized Fourier coefficients are exactly normalized sums on an integer residue class.
- **stmt-prop-scale-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-scale-transfer)): There are constants $C_{\rm tr}<\infty$ and $N_{\rm tr}$ with the following property. Let $N\ge N_{\rm tr}$, let $A\subseteq[N]$ be square-difference-free, and let $B\subseteq\mathcal P_N$ satisfy $q_B\le N^\tau$. Put $$\mathcal S=\mathcal P_N\setminus B,\qquad D=M_0q_B,\qquad N'=\lceil N/D^2\rceil,\qquad \lambda=\frac{D^2N'}N.$$ For any $s\bmod M_0$ and $z_B\in X_B$, define $$g=(\mathcal L_{[N],\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s)(z_B,\cdot).$$ Then $1\le N'<N$, and $$Z_{\mathcal S}(g) \le \lambda^d \max_{\substack{A'\subseteq[N']\\ A'\ {\rm square\text{-}difference\text{-}free}}} Y(N',A') +C_{\rm tr}N^{-\sigma_0}. \label{eq:scale-transfer-diagonal}$$ Moreover, for arbitrary choices $s_v\bmod M_0$ and $z_{B,v}\in X_B$, one for every $v\in V$, the functions $$g_v=(\mathcal L_{[N],\mathcal P_N,Q}f_{s_v})(z_{B,v},\cdot)$$ satisfy $$\|\mathcal Z_{\mathcal S}((g_v)_{v\in V})\| \le \lambda^d \max_{\substack{A'\subseteq[N']\\ A'\ {\rm square\text{-}difference\text{-}free}}} Y(N',A') +C_{\rm tr}N^{-\sigma_0}. \label{eq:scale-transfer-mixed}$$ All constants are uniform in $N,A,B,s,z_B$ and the mixed specifications. Finally, $$1\le\lambda<1+\frac{D^2}{N},\qquad \lambda^d=1+O_d(D^2/N) =1+O_{d,M_0}(N^{-1+2\tau}). \label{eq:scale-transfer-factor}$$
  - Explanation: Every permitted residue specification compares to a shorter interval through square-step progressions. The estimate includes mixed inputs and a uniform ceiling error, without an induction hypothesis.
- **stmt-lem-good-residues** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-good-residues)): The proportion $\rho$ defined in <a href="#eq:good-residue-proportion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-residue-proportion">[eq:good-residue-proportion]</a> satisfies $$\rho\ge M_0^{-23}>0.$$
  - Explanation: A fixed positive proportion of residue assignments has strict-square differences along the designated cycle.
- **stmt-prop-nonexceptional-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-nonexceptional-contraction)): There are absolute constants $C$ and $N_0$ such that, whenever $N\ge N_0$, $A\subseteq[N]$ has no positive square difference, and $\boldsymbol s$ is good, one has $$\left\|\int\prod_{v\in V} \mathcal L_{[N],\mathcal P_N,Q}f_{s_v}(z_v) \,d\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P_N}(\mu_p-\varepsilon_p)\right\| \le C N^{-\sigma_0}. \label{eq:nonexceptional-contraction}$$ The constants are uniform in $A$ and $\boldsymbol s$.
  - Explanation: Good residue assignments make the nonexceptional multilinear contribution power-decaying, uniformly over square-difference-free sets.
- **stmt-prop-induction-recurrence** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-induction-recurrence)): There are absolute constants $K<\infty$ and $N_1\ge2$ such that, for every integer $N\ge N_1$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal Y(N) &\le K N^{-\sigma_0} +(1+u_N)\left((1-\rho)\mathcal Y(n_\varnothing) +\rho\! \sum_{\substack{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N\\q_B\le N^\tau}} \eta_B\mathcal Y(n_B)\right), \label{eq:functional-recurrence}\\ u_N&=(1+M_0^2N^{2\tau-1})^d-1. \label{eq:uniform-ceiling-error} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, $u_N\to0$ as $N\to\infty$. The constants $K,N_1$ do not depend on any proposed induction bound for $\mathcal Y$.
  - Explanation: A weighted recurrence combines scale transfer on nongood assignments with nonexceptional cancellation and exceptional subset contributions on good assignments.
- **stmt-thm-functional-decay** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-functional-decay)): There are absolute constants $a_0>0$ and $C_0<\infty$ such that, for every positive integer $N$ and every square-difference-free set $A\subseteq[N]$, $$Y(N,A)\le C_0N^{-a_0}.$$
  - Explanation: The nonnegative functional decays by a fixed absolute power for all square-difference-free sets and all positive interval lengths.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-marked-positive",
        "exact_quote": "Put $$\\kappa=\\frac9{10}\\delta^{h-1},\\qquad\n \\lambda=(e^\\kappa-1)^n,\\qquad\n c_* =\\lambda m_{t_0+1}^{-n}>0.$$ For every $S$ with $|S|\\le t_0+1$, every cut in a marked block $b\\in S$, and every real $F:\\mathbb F_p^{V_+}\\to\\mathbb R$, $$Q_{p,S}^{b}(F)\n \\ge \\lambda m_s^{-n}\\,\n \\mathbb E_{Y,j_+}I_S(Y)\n F((Y_{u_S(v),j_v})_{v\\in V_+})^2.\n \\label{eq:tuple-marked-lower}$$ Here the expectation uses all uniform lists and only the uniform indices on $V_+$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-unmarked-error",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b\\notin S$ specify a cut and suppose $s=|S|\\le t_0$. Let $Y_+$ consist of the lists at plus addresses, and let $I_+(Y_+)$ impose only the constraints internal to those addresses.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-conditional-mixing",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed index set $V$ and the measures constructed above, there is an absolute threshold $P$ such that the following statements hold for every prime $p\\ge P$. Both probability measures $$\\mu_p,\\qquad\n \\mu_p^\\circ=\\frac{\\mu_p-\\varepsilon_p}{1-\\eta_p},\n \\qquad \\eta_p=\\varepsilon_p(1),$$ have uniform marginals in each individual label. If $\\rho$ is either measure, $v\\in V$, and $\\lambda$ is its marginal on $\\mathbb F_p^{V\\setminus\\{v\\}}$, then every function $f:\\mathbb F_p\\to\\mathbb C$ with $\\mathbb E_x f(x)=0$ satisfies $$\\left\\|\\mathbb E_\\rho\\bigl[f(z_v)\\mid(z_w)_{w\\ne v}\\bigr]\n \\right\\|_{L^2(\\lambda)}\n \\le p^{-1/64}\\|f\\|_{L^2(\\mathbb F_p)}.\n \\label{eq:tuple-conditional-mixing}$$ The threshold is uniform in the choice of $v$ and $\\rho$.*\n\n\n*For every selected cycle edge $v_j\\to v_{j+1}$, its pair marginal under $\\mu_p^\\circ$ is uniform on $$\\Omega_p=\\{(x,y)\\in\\mathbb F_p^2:y-x\\in Q_p^*\\}.$$ Its pair marginal under $\\mu_p$ is $$(1-\\eta_p)\\operatorname{Unif}(\\Omega_p)\n +\\eta_p\\operatorname{Unif}(\\mathbb F_p^2).\n \\label{eq:tuple-full-pair-law}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-conditional-mixing",
          "exact_quote": "For the fixed index set $V$ and the measures constructed above, there is an absolute threshold $P$ such that the following statements hold for every prime $p\\ge P$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-holder",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-functional-properties",
        "exact_quote": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$, and $$|\\mathbb E g|\\le\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:functional-mean}$$ For $a_p\\in\\mathbb F_p$ and $r_p\\in Q_p^*$, let $A(x)_p=r_px_p+a_p$. Then $$\\|g\\circ A\\|_{\\mathcal P}=\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:functional-affine}$$ For every $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P$, $$\\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{\\mathcal P\\setminus B}\n   =\\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{\\mathcal P}\n   \\le\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:functional-projection}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-functional-properties",
          "exact_quote": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$, and $$|\\mathbb E g|\\le\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:functional-mean}$$ For $a_p\\in\\mathbb F_p$ and $r_p\\in Q_p^*$, let $A(x)_p=r_px_p+a_p$. Then $$\\|g\\circ A\\|_{\\mathcal P}=\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:functional-affine}$$ For every $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P$, $$\\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{\\mathcal P\\setminus B}\n   =\\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{\\mathcal P}\n   \\le\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:functional-projection}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$, and $$|\\mathbb E g|\\le\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:functional-mean}$$ For $a_p\\in\\mathbb F_p$ and $r_p\\in Q_p^*$, let $A(x)_p=r_px_p+a_p$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-conductor-decay",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be a finite set of primes at least $P$, and at each prime choose $\\rho_p$ to be either $\\mu_p$ or $\\mu_p^\\circ$. Then $$\\left|\\int\\prod_{w\\in V}g_w(z_w)\\,\n       d\\!\\bigotimes_{p\\in\\mathcal P}\\rho_p(z)\\right|\n \\le R^{-\\gamma}\\|g_v\\|_2\n        \\prod_{w\\ne v}\\|g_w\\|_{2(d-1)}.\n \\label{eq:conductor-decay}$$ All norms on the right use uniform probability measure on $X_{\\mathcal P}$. Then $$\\left|\\int\\prod_{w\\in V}g_w(z_w)\\,\n       d\\!\\bigotimes_{p\\in\\mathcal P}\\rho_p(z)\\right|\n \\le R^{-\\gamma}\\|g_v\\|_2\n        \\prod_{w\\ne v}\\|g_w\\|_{2(d-1)}.\n \\label{eq:conductor-decay}$$ All norms on the right use uniform probability measure on $X_{\\mathcal P}$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lift-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q\\ge1$, let $I$ have length $L\\ge10Q^6$, and suppose $\\|f\\|_\\infty\\le M$. Let $\\mathcal F$ be any subfamily of $\\{U\\subseteq\\mathcal P:q_U\\le Q\\}$, and put $$k=\\max\\{|U|:U\\subseteq\\mathcal P,\\ q_U\\le Q\\}.$$ For every integer $r\\ge1$, define $$K_{2r}=\\left(\\frac{(2r)!}{2^r r!}\\right)^{1/(2r)},\n \\qquad\n A_r=2eK_{2r}\\,2^{(r+3)/4}.$$ Then $$\\left\\|\\mathcal L^{\\mathcal F}_{I,\\mathcal P,Q}f\\right\\|_{2r}\n \\le 2M(k+1)A_r^k.\n \\label{eq:lift-explicit-moment}$$ Consequently, for fixed $r$, this norm is at most $M Q^{o_r(1)}$ as $Q\\to\\infty$, uniformly in $I,f,\\mathcal P$, and $\\mathcal F$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lift-moments",

          "source_id": "PM-182.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lift-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P$, let $z_B\\in X_B$, and let $G:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb C$. For every finite $p\\ge1$, $$\\|G(z_B,\\cdot)\\|_{L^p(X_{\\mathcal P\\setminus B})}\n \\le q_B^{1/p}\\|G\\|_{L^p(X_{\\mathcal P})}.\n \\label{eq:lift-specialization}$$ In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:lift-moments\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:lift-moments\">15</a>, this gives, for every fixed $z_B$, $$\\left\\|\\bigl(\\mathcal L^{\\mathcal F}_{I,\\mathcal P,Q}f\\bigr)\n                  (z_B,\\cdot)\\right\\|_{2r}\n \\le 2M q_B^{1/(2r)}(k+1)A_r^k.$$ The same bound holds for either part obtained by cutting the remaining denominator at any $H\\ge1$: before specialization these parts correspond to the whole-support families $$\\mathcal F_{\\le H,B}\n  =\\{U\\in\\mathcal F:q_{U\\setminus B}\\le H\\},\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal F_{>H,B}\n  =\\{U\\in\\mathcal F:q_{U\\setminus B}>H\\}.$$ After specialization their Fourier supports lie, respectively, at remaining denominators at most $H$ and greater than $H$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lift-specialization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P$, let $z_B\\in X_B$, and let $G:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb C$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-square-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N\\ge1$ be an integer, put $H=N^{\\beta/2}$, and let $D=8d_0\\le N^\\beta$, where $d_0$ is odd and squarefree. Suppose $F,J:[N]\\to\\mathbb C$ satisfy $|F|,|J|\\le1$ and are supported in residue classes $a,b$ modulo $D$, respectively. Assume that $b-a$ is a strict square class, that $x<y$ whenever $x\\in\\operatorname{supp}F$ and $y\\in\\operatorname{supp}J$, and that no such difference $y-x$ is the square of a positive integer. With $$\\widehat F(\\xi)=\\frac1N\\sum_{n=1}^N F(n)e(-n\\xi),\n \\qquad\n \\widehat J(\\xi)=\\frac1N\\sum_{n=1}^N J(n)e(-n\\xi),$$ one has $$D\\left|\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\xi\\in\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z:\\ q(\\xi)\\le H\\\\\n                  q(\\xi)\\ \\mathrm{squarefree},\\ (q(\\xi),D)=1}}\n      \\mathfrak g(\\xi)\\widehat F(-\\xi)\\widehat J(\\xi)\n \\right|\n \\ll H^{-1/3}.\n \\label{eq:kernel-conclusion}$$ The implied constant is absolute and is independent of all the data in the statement.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-square-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N\\ge1$ be an integer, put $H=N^{\\beta/2}$, and let $D=8d_0\\le N^\\beta$, where $d_0$ is odd and squarefree. Assume that $b-a$ is a strict square class, that $x<y$ whenever $x\\in\\operatorname{supp}F$ and $y\\in\\operatorname{supp}J$, and that no such difference $y-x$ is the square of a positive integer. With $$\\widehat F(\\xi)=\\frac1N\\sum_{n=1}^N F(n)e(-n\\xi),\n \\qquad\n \\widehat J(\\xi)=\\frac1N\\sum_{n=1}^N J(n)e(-n\\xi),$$ one has $$D\\left|\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\xi\\in\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z:\\ q(\\xi)\\le H\\\\\n                  q(\\xi)\\ \\mathrm{squarefree},\\ (q(\\xi),D)=1}}\n      \\mathfrak g(\\xi)\\widehat F(-\\xi)\\widehat J(\\xi)\n \\right|\n \\ll H^{-1/3}.\n \\label{eq:kernel-conclusion}$$ The implied constant is absolute and is independent of all the data in the statement.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $N\\ge1$ be an integer, put $H=N^{\\beta/2}$, and let $D=8d_0\\le N^\\beta$, where $d_0$ is odd and squarefree. Suppose $F,J:[N]\\to\\mathbb C$ satisfy $|F|,|J|\\le1$ and are supported in residue classes $a,b$ modulo $D$, respectively. Assume that $b-a$ is a strict square class, that $x<y$ whenever $x\\in\\operatorname{supp}F$ and $y\\in\\operatorname{supp}J$, and that no such difference $y-x$ is the square of a positive integer. With $$\\widehat F(\\xi)=\\frac1N\\sum_{n=1}^N F(n)e(-n\\xi),\n \\qquad\n \\widehat J(\\xi)=\\frac1N\\sum_{n=1}^N J(n)e(-n\\xi),$$ one has $$D\\left|\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\xi\\in\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z:\\ q(\\xi)\\le H\\\\\n                  q(\\xi)\\ \\mathrm{squarefree},\\ (q(\\xi),D)=1}}\n      \\mathfrak g(\\xi)\\widehat F(-\\xi)\\widehat J(\\xi)\n \\right|\n \\ll H^{-1/3}.\n \\label{eq:kernel-conclusion}$$ The implied constant is absolute and is independent of all the data in the statement.",
      "explanation": "Ordered integer supports with strict-square residue differences and no actual positive square difference yield a small low-denominator quadratic Fourier sum.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exact-fiber",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N\\ge1$, let $A\\subseteq[N]$, and define $f_s$ by <a href=\"#eq:small-residue-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-residue-input\">[eq:small-residue-input]</a>. Let $I\\subseteq[N]$ be a nonempty interval, let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$, and put $$q_B=\\prod_{p\\in B}p,\\qquad\n \\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B,\\qquad D=M_0q_B.$$ Fix $s\\bmod M_0$ and $z_B\\in X_B$, and let $a\\bmod D$ be their combined residue class. If $$g_I=(\\mathcal L_{I,\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s)(z_B,\\cdot),$$ then every character $\\xi$ on $X_{\\mathcal S}$ satisfying $q(\\xi)q_B\\le Q$ has coefficient $$\\widehat g_I(\\xi)\n =\\frac D{|I|}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{n\\in I\\\\n\\equiv a\\pmod D}}\n       1_A(n)e(-n\\xi).\n \\label{eq:interval-fiber-coefficient}$$ Here $q_\\varnothing=1$, and the hat denotes the normalized Fourier coefficient on the remaining product space.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-exact-fiber",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N\\ge1$, let $A\\subseteq[N]$, and define $f_s$ by <a href=\"#eq:small-residue-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-residue-input\">[eq:small-residue-input]</a>. Let $I\\subseteq[N]$ be a nonempty interval, let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$, and put $$q_B=\\prod_{p\\in B}p,\\qquad\n \\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B,\\qquad D=M_0q_B.$$ Fix $s\\bmod M_0$ and $z_B\\in X_B$, and let $a\\bmod D$ be their combined residue class. If $$g_I=(\\mathcal L_{I,\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s)(z_B,\\cdot),$$ then every character $\\xi$ on $X_{\\mathcal S}$ satisfying $q(\\xi)q_B\\le Q$ has coefficient $$\\widehat g_I(\\xi)\n =\\frac D{|I|}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{n\\in I\\\\n\\equiv a\\pmod D}}\n       1_A(n)e(-n\\xi).\n \\label{eq:interval-fiber-coefficient}$$ Here $q_\\varnothing=1$, and the hat denotes the normalized Fourier coefficient on the remaining product space.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $N\\ge1$, let $A\\subseteq[N]$, and define $f_s$ by <a href=\"#eq:small-residue-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-residue-input\">[eq:small-residue-input]</a>. Let $I\\subseteq[N]$ be a nonempty interval, let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$, and put $$q_B=\\prod_{p\\in B}p,\\qquad\n \\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B,\\qquad D=M_0q_B.$$ Fix $s\\bmod M_0$ and $z_B\\in X_B$, and let $a\\bmod D$ be their combined residue class. If $$g_I=(\\mathcal L_{I,\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s)(z_B,\\cdot),$$ then every character $\\xi$ on $X_{\\mathcal S}$ satisfying $q(\\xi)q_B\\le Q$ has coefficient $$\\widehat g_I(\\xi)\n =\\frac D{|I|}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{n\\in I\\\\n\\equiv a\\pmod D}}\n       1_A(n)e(-n\\xi).\n \\label{eq:interval-fiber-coefficient}$$ Here $q_\\varnothing=1$, and the hat denotes the normalized Fourier coefficient on the remaining product space.",
      "explanation": "When the entire fixed-coordinate character group fits below the cutoff, specialized Fourier coefficients are exactly normalized sums on an integer residue class.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-182.3"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scale-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $C_{\\rm tr}<\\infty$ and $N_{\\rm tr}$ with the following property. Let $N\\ge N_{\\rm tr}$, let $A\\subseteq[N]$ be square-difference-free, and let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$ satisfy $q_B\\le N^\\tau$. Put $$\\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B,\\qquad\n D=M_0q_B,\\qquad N'=\\lceil N/D^2\\rceil,\\qquad\n \\lambda=\\frac{D^2N'}N.$$ For any $s\\bmod M_0$ and $z_B\\in X_B$, define $$g=(\\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s)(z_B,\\cdot).$$ Then $1\\le N'<N$, and $$Z_{\\mathcal S}(g)\n \\le \\lambda^d\n       \\max_{\\substack{A'\\subseteq[N']\\\\\n                       A'\\ {\\rm square\\text{-}difference\\text{-}free}}}\n                Y(N',A')\n       +C_{\\rm tr}N^{-\\sigma_0}.\n \\label{eq:scale-transfer-diagonal}$$ Moreover, for arbitrary choices $s_v\\bmod M_0$ and $z_{B,v}\\in X_B$, one for every $v\\in V$, the functions $$g_v=(\\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_{s_v})(z_{B,v},\\cdot)$$ satisfy $$|\\mathcal Z_{\\mathcal S}((g_v)_{v\\in V})|\n \\le \\lambda^d\n       \\max_{\\substack{A'\\subseteq[N']\\\\\n                       A'\\ {\\rm square\\text{-}difference\\text{-}free}}}\n                Y(N',A')\n       +C_{\\rm tr}N^{-\\sigma_0}.\n \\label{eq:scale-transfer-mixed}$$ All constants are uniform in $N,A,B,s,z_B$ and the mixed specifications. Finally, $$1\\le\\lambda<1+\\frac{D^2}{N},\\qquad\n \\lambda^d=1+O_d(D^2/N)\n           =1+O_{d,M_0}(N^{-1+2\\tau}).\n \\label{eq:scale-transfer-factor}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-scale-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $C_{\\rm tr}<\\infty$ and $N_{\\rm tr}$ with the following property. Let $N\\ge N_{\\rm tr}$, let $A\\subseteq[N]$ be square-difference-free, and let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$ satisfy $q_B\\le N^\\tau$. Put $$\\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B,\\qquad\n D=M_0q_B,\\qquad N'=\\lceil N/D^2\\rceil,\\qquad\n \\lambda=\\frac{D^2N'}N.$$ For any $s\\bmod M_0$ and $z_B\\in X_B$, define $$g=(\\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s)(z_B,\\cdot).$$ Then $1\\le N'<N$, and $$Z_{\\mathcal S}(g)\n \\le \\lambda^d\n       \\max_{\\substack{A'\\subseteq[N']\\\\\n                       A'\\ {\\rm square\\text{-}difference\\text{-}free}}}\n                Y(N',A')\n       +C_{\\rm tr}N^{-\\sigma_0}.\n \\label{eq:scale-transfer-diagonal}$$ Moreover, for arbitrary choices $s_v\\bmod M_0$ and $z_{B,v}\\in X_B$, one for every $v\\in V$, the functions $$g_v=(\\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_{s_v})(z_{B,v},\\cdot)$$ satisfy $$|\\mathcal Z_{\\mathcal S}((g_v)_{v\\in V})|\n \\le \\lambda^d\n       \\max_{\\substack{A'\\subseteq[N']\\\\\n                       A'\\ {\\rm square\\text{-}difference\\text{-}free}}}\n                Y(N',A')\n       +C_{\\rm tr}N^{-\\sigma_0}.\n \\label{eq:scale-transfer-mixed}$$ All constants are uniform in $N,A,B,s,z_B$ and the mixed specifications. Finally, $$1\\le\\lambda<1+\\frac{D^2}{N},\\qquad\n \\lambda^d=1+O_d(D^2/N)\n           =1+O_{d,M_0}(N^{-1+2\\tau}).\n \\label{eq:scale-transfer-factor}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are constants $C_{\\rm tr}<\\infty$ and $N_{\\rm tr}$ with the following property. Let $N\\ge N_{\\rm tr}$, let $A\\subseteq[N]$ be square-difference-free, and let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$ satisfy $q_B\\le N^\\tau$. Put $$\\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B,\\qquad\n D=M_0q_B,\\qquad N'=\\lceil N/D^2\\rceil,\\qquad\n \\lambda=\\frac{D^2N'}N.$$ For any $s\\bmod M_0$ and $z_B\\in X_B$, define $$g=(\\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s)(z_B,\\cdot).$$ Then $1\\le N'<N$, and $$Z_{\\mathcal S}(g)\n \\le \\lambda^d\n       \\max_{\\substack{A'\\subseteq[N']\\\\\n                       A'\\ {\\rm square\\text{-}difference\\text{-}free}}}\n                Y(N',A')\n       +C_{\\rm tr}N^{-\\sigma_0}.\n \\label{eq:scale-transfer-diagonal}$$ Moreover, for arbitrary choices $s_v\\bmod M_0$ and $z_{B,v}\\in X_B$, one for every $v\\in V$, the functions $$g_v=(\\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_{s_v})(z_{B,v},\\cdot)$$ satisfy $$|\\mathcal Z_{\\mathcal S}((g_v)_{v\\in V})|\n \\le \\lambda^d\n       \\max_{\\substack{A'\\subseteq[N']\\\\\n                       A'\\ {\\rm square\\text{-}difference\\text{-}free}}}\n                Y(N',A')\n       +C_{\\rm tr}N^{-\\sigma_0}.\n \\label{eq:scale-transfer-mixed}$$ All constants are uniform in $N,A,B,s,z_B$ and the mixed specifications.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-good-residues",
        "exact_quote": "The proportion $\\rho$ defined in <a href=\"#eq:good-residue-proportion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:good-residue-proportion\">[eq:good-residue-proportion]</a> satisfies $$\\rho\\ge M_0^{-23}>0.$$",

      "evidence": [

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Integer interval** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a positive integer $N$, write $[N]=\{1,\ldots,N\}$.
- **Square-difference-free set** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A set $A\subseteq[N]$ is *square-difference-free* if $$(A-A)\cap\{m^2:m\in\mathbb N,\ m\ge1\}=\varnothing.$$
- **Extremal cardinality** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Define $$s(N)=\max\{\|A\|:A\subseteq[N]\text{ is square-difference-free}\}.$$
- **Strict-square indicator and discrepancy** ([source](#sec:finite-field-graphs)): For an odd prime $p$, write $$Q_p^*=\{u^2:u\in\mathbb F_p^\times\}, \qquad A_p(t)=1_{Q_p^*}(t), \qquad \delta_p=\frac{\sqrt p+1}{2p}\le p^{-1/2}.$$
- **Kernel operator** ([source](#sec:finite-field-graphs)): For finite sets $X,Y$, the operator associated with a kernel $K:X\times Y\to\mathbb R$ is $$T_K f(x)=\mathbb E_{y\in Y}K(x,y)f(y).$$
- **Tuple index set** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Write $$K=h!,\qquad r=K/2,\qquad V=\Pi^\ell,\qquad d=\|V\|=K^\ell, \qquad n=d/2.$$
- **Reflection positivity** ([source](#pm-h-8)): We say that $\nu$ is *reflection positive* if this matrix is symmetric positive semidefinite for every cut above.
- **Component measure** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Define the finite measure $\nu_{p,S}$ by the identity $$\int G(z)\,d\nu_{p,S}(z) =\mathbb E_Y\mathbb E_j I_S(Y)W_S(j) G\bigl((Y_{u_S(v),j_v})_{v\in V}\bigr) \label{eq:tuple-component-law}$$ for all functions $G$ on $\mathbb F_p^V$.
- **Product prime space and law** ([source](#sec:functional)): For a finite set $\mathcal P$ of primes at least $P$, define $$X_{\mathcal P}=\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}\mathbb F_p, \qquad \mu_{\mathcal P}=\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P}\mu_p.$$
- **Multilinear and diagonal functionals** ([source](#sec:functional)): Set $$\mathcal Z_{\mathcal P}((g_v)_{v\in V}) =\int\prod_{v\in V}g_v(z_v)\,d\mu_{\mathcal P}(z), \qquad Z_{\mathcal P}(g)=\mathcal Z_{\mathcal P}((g)_{v\in V}). \label{eq:functional-definition}$$
- **Signed seminorm** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Proposition <a href="#prop:signed-holder" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:signed-holder">11</a> allows us to define, for real $g$, $$\\|g\\|_{\mathcal P}=Z_{\mathcal P}(g)^{1/d}.$$
- **Conductor** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The *conductor* of a rational frequency is its reduced positive denominator; the conductor of $0$ is $1$.
- **Fourier lift** ([source](#sec:lift-moments)): For $Q\ge1$, the Fourier lift is $$\mathcal L_{I,\mathcal P,Q}f =\sum_{\substack{U\subseteq\mathcal P\\q_U\le Q}}f_U.$$
- **Unit quadratic multiplier** ([source](#sec:square-kernel)): For every rational frequency define the normalized unit quadratic sum $$\mathfrak g(\xi) =\frac{1}{\varphi(q(\xi))} \sum_{m\in(\mathbb Z/q(\xi)\mathbb Z)^\times}e(\xi m^2), \qquad \mathfrak g(0)=1. \label{eq:kernel-unit-gauss}$$
- **Strict square class** ([source](#sec:square-kernel)): If $D=8d_0$ with $d_0$ odd and squarefree, call a residue modulo $D$ a *strict square class* if it is $1$ modulo $8$ and a nonzero square modulo every prime dividing $d_0$.
- **Small-residue input** ([source](#sec:scale-transfer)): We also set $$f_s(n)=M_0\,1_{\{n\equiv s\pmod{M_0}\}}1_A(n) \quad(s\in\mathbb Z/M_0\mathbb Z), \qquad 1_A=\mathbb E_{s\bmod M_0}f_s. \label{eq:small-residue-input}$$
- **Induction functional** ([source](#sec:scale-transfer)): For a square-difference-free set $A\subseteq[N]$, the quantity that will eventually be estimated by induction is $$Y(N,A)= Z_{\mathcal P_N}\bigl(\mathcal L_{[N],\mathcal P_N,Q}1_A\bigr). \label{eq:induction-functional}$$
- **Good residue assignment** ([source](#sec:ordered-contraction)): A tuple of residue classes $\boldsymbol s=(s_v)_{v\in V}\in(\mathbb Z/M_0\mathbb Z)^V$ is called *good* if $$s_{v_{j+1}}-s_{v_j} \quad\text{is a strict square class modulo }M_0 \qquad(0\le j<24),$$ where $v_{24}=v_0$.
- **Maximal functional** ([source](#sec:induction)): For every positive integer $n$, define $$\mathcal Y(n)= \max_{\substack{A\subseteq[n]\\ A\text{ square-difference-free}}}Y(n,A). \label{eq:maximal-functional}$$

      "exact_definition": "Proposition <a href=\"#prop:signed-holder\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:signed-holder\">11</a> allows us to define, for real $g$, $$\\|g\\|_{\\mathcal P}=Z_{\\mathcal P}(g)^{1/d}.$$",
      "interpretation": "The nonnegative root of the diagonal form measures real functions.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Signed seminorm",
      "scope": "Real functions on the product prime space."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-5",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-5",
          "exact_quote": "The *conductor* of a rational frequency is its reduced positive denominator; the conductor of $0$ is $1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "The *conductor* of a rational frequency is its reduced positive denominator; the conductor of $0$ is $1$.",
      "interpretation": "A rational frequency is indexed by its reduced positive denominator.",
      "kind": "notation",
      "name": "Conductor",
      "scope": "Rational frequencies modulo the integers."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:lift-moments",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:lift-moments",
          "exact_quote": "For $Q\\ge1$, the Fourier lift is $$\\mathcal L_{I,\\mathcal P,Q}f\n   =\\sum_{\\substack{U\\subseteq\\mathcal P\\\\q_U\\le Q}}f_U.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For $Q\\ge1$, the Fourier lift is $$\\mathcal L_{I,\\mathcal P,Q}f\n   =\\sum_{\\substack{U\\subseteq\\mathcal P\\\\q_U\\le Q}}f_U.$$",
      "interpretation": "Complete frequency classes of bounded exact denominator are retained from interval Fourier coefficients.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Fourier lift",
      "scope": "A nonempty integer interval and a finite prime set."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:square-kernel",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:square-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "For every rational frequency define the normalized unit quadratic sum $$\\mathfrak g(\\xi)\n =\\frac{1}{\\varphi(q(\\xi))}\n   \\sum_{m\\in(\\mathbb Z/q(\\xi)\\mathbb Z)^\\times}e(\\xi m^2),\n \\qquad \\mathfrak g(0)=1.\n \\label{eq:kernel-unit-gauss}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For every rational frequency define the normalized unit quadratic sum $$\\mathfrak g(\\xi)\n =\\frac{1}{\\varphi(q(\\xi))}\n   \\sum_{m\\in(\\mathbb Z/q(\\xi)\\mathbb Z)^\\times}e(\\xi m^2),\n \\qquad \\mathfrak g(0)=1.\n \\label{eq:kernel-unit-gauss}$$",
      "interpretation": "The multiplier averages the quadratic phase over units of the reduced denominator.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Unit quadratic multiplier",
      "scope": "Rational frequencies modulo the integers."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:square-kernel",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:square-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "If $D=8d_0$ with $d_0$ odd and squarefree, call a residue modulo $D$ a *strict square class* if it is $1$ modulo $8$ and a nonzero square modulo every prime dividing $d_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "If $D=8d_0$ with $d_0$ odd and squarefree, call a residue modulo $D$ a *strict square class* if it is $1$ modulo $8$ and a nonzero square modulo every prime dividing $d_0$.",
      "interpretation": "A residue meets the prescribed odd-prime nonzero-square conditions and the condition at the power of two.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Strict square class",
      "scope": "Moduli of the stated form."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:scale-transfer",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:scale-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "We also set $$f_s(n)=M_0\\,1_{\\{n\\equiv s\\pmod{M_0}\\}}1_A(n)\n \\quad(s\\in\\mathbb Z/M_0\\mathbb Z),\n \\qquad\n 1_A=\\mathbb E_{s\\bmod M_0}f_s.\n \\label{eq:small-residue-input}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "We also set $$f_s(n)=M_0\\,1_{\\{n\\equiv s\\pmod{M_0}\\}}1_A(n)\n \\quad(s\\in\\mathbb Z/M_0\\mathbb Z),\n \\qquad\n 1_A=\\mathbb E_{s\\bmod M_0}f_s.\n \\label{eq:small-residue-input}$$",
      "interpretation": "Scaled residue-class restrictions average back to the original set indicator.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Small-residue input",
      "scope": "Residues modulo the fixed small modulus."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:scale-transfer",

          "anchor": "sec:scale-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "For a square-difference-free set $A\\subseteq[N]$, the quantity that will eventually be estimated by induction is $$Y(N,A)=\n Z_{\\mathcal P_N}\\bigl(\\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}1_A\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:induction-functional}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For a square-difference-free set $A\\subseteq[N]$, the quantity that will eventually be estimated by induction is $$Y(N,A)=\n Z_{\\mathcal P_N}\\bigl(\\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}1_A\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:induction-functional}$$",
      "interpretation": "The diagonal tuple functional is applied to the interval indicator lift.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Induction functional",
      "scope": "Square-difference-free integer sets."
    },

        {
          "anchor": "sec:ordered-contraction",
          "exact_quote": "A tuple of residue classes $\\boldsymbol s=(s_v)_{v\\in V}\\in(\\mathbb Z/M_0\\mathbb Z)^V$ is called *good* if $$s_{v_{j+1}}-s_{v_j}\n \\quad\\text{is a strict square class modulo }M_0\n \\qquad(0\\le j<24),$$ where $v_{24}=v_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A tuple of residue classes $\\boldsymbol s=(s_v)_{v\\in V}\\in(\\mathbb Z/M_0\\mathbb Z)^V$ is called *good* if $$s_{v_{j+1}}-s_{v_j}\n \\quad\\text{is a strict square class modulo }M_0\n \\qquad(0\\le j<24),$$ where $v_{24}=v_0$.",
      "interpretation": "Every selected cycle edge has a strict-square residue difference.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Good residue assignment",
      "scope": "Tuples modulo the fixed small modulus."

          "source_id": "PM-182.3"

      "interpretation": "The functional is maximized over square-difference-free subsets at a given scale.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-count",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be an odd prime and let $G=(W,E)$ be a finite directed graph with no loops and at most one edge on each unordered pair of distinct vertices. Put $m=|E|$, and let $(z_w)_{w\\in W}$ be independent uniform elements of $\\mathbb F_p$. Then $$\\left|\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{(u,v)\\in E}A_p(z_v-z_u)-2^{-m}\n \\right|\n \\le m\\delta_p.$$ The assertion includes the empty graph, whose product is $1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "graph-count-error",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "Graph constraint discrepancy",
      "regime": {

    {

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-list-mixing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $I,J$ be finite disjoint sets, and let $G$ be a directed graph on $I\\sqcup J$ whose $m$ edges all join $I$ to $J$, with at most one edge on each unordered pair. The directions may vary from edge to edge. For $x\\in\\mathbb F_p^I$, $y\\in\\mathbb F_p^J$, write $z_i=x_i$ for $i\\in I$, $z_j=y_j$ for $j\\in J$, and set $$K(x,y)=\\prod_{(u,v)\\in E(G)}A_p(z_v-z_u).$$ Then $$\\|K-2^{-m}\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n \\le (32m)^{1/4}p^{-1/8}.\n \\label{eq:list-mixing}$$ For $m=0$, both sides are zero. In particular, the implied constant for any fixed pair of lists is independent of $p$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "list-mixing-error",
      "kind": "bound",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tuple-laws",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute integer $P$ such that, for every prime $p\\ge P$, there are a probability measure $\\mu_p$ on $\\mathbb F_p^V$ and a nonnegative submeasure $\\varepsilon_p\\le\\mu_p$ whose mass satisfies $$\\eta_p=\\varepsilon_p(\\mathbb F_p^V)\\le p^{-4}.$$ Writing $$\\mu_p^\\circ=\\frac{\\mu_p-\\varepsilon_p}{1-\\eta_p},$$ the following properties hold.*\n\n1.  *The full law $\\mu_p$ is reflection positive. Both $\\mu_p$ and $\\varepsilon_p$ are invariant under simultaneous changes $z_v\\mapsto qz_v+a$, where $a\\in\\mathbb F_p$ and $q\\in Q_p^*$. Every single-label marginal of $\\mu_p$ and $\\mu_p^\\circ$ is uniform on $\\mathbb F_p$.*\n\n2.  *On the support of $\\mu_p^\\circ$, $$z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}\\in Q_p^*\\qquad(0\\le j<h).$$ The marginal of each ordered pair $(z_{v_j},z_{v_{j+1}})$ under $\\mu_p^\\circ$ is uniform on $\\{(x,y):y-x\\in Q_p^*\\}$.*\n\n\n3.  *Put $\\gamma=1/64$. For either $\\rho_p=\\mu_p$ or $\\rho_p=\\mu_p^\\circ$, every $v\\in V$, and every $f:\\mathbb F_p\\to\\mathbb C$ with uniform mean zero, $$\\left\\|\\mathbb E_{\\rho_p}\n     \\bigl[f(z_v)\\mid(z_w)_{w\\ne v}\\bigr]\\right\\|_2\n     \\le p^{-\\gamma}\\|f\\|_2.\n     \\label{eq:tuple-conditional-bound}$$ The norm on the left uses the other-label marginal of $\\rho_p$; the norm on the right uses uniform measure on $\\mathbb F_p$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "exceptional-mass",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "\\eta_p",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Prime laws beyond the absolute threshold.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-prop-tuple-laws",

For either $\\rho_p=\\mu_p$ or $\\rho_p=\\mu_p^\\circ$, every $v\\in V$, and every $f:\\mathbb F_p\\to\\mathbb C$ with uniform mean zero, $$\\left\\|\\mathbb E_{\\rho_p}\n     \\bigl[f(z_v)\\mid(z_w)_{w\\ne v}\\bigr]\\right\\|_2\n     \\le p^{-\\gamma}\\|f\\|_2.\n     \\label{eq:tuple-conditional-bound}$$ The norm on the left uses the other-label marginal of $\\rho_p$; the norm on the right uses uniform measure on $\\mathbb F_p$.",
            "source_id": "PM-182.3"
          }

        "quantifiers": [

            "anchor": "stmt-prop-tuple-laws",
            "exact_quote": "There is an absolute integer $P$ such that, for every prime $p\\ge P$, there are a probability measure $\\mu_p$ on $\\mathbb F_p^V$ and a nonnegative submeasure $\\varepsilon_p\\le\\mu_p$ whose mass satisfies $$\\eta_p=\\varepsilon_p(\\mathbb F_p^V)\\le p^{-4}.$$ Writing $$\\mu_p^\\circ=\\frac{\\mu_p-\\varepsilon_p}{1-\\eta_p},$$ the following properties hold.*\n\n1.  *The full law $\\mu_p$ is reflection positive. Both $\\mu_p$ and $\\varepsilon_p$ are invariant under simultaneous changes $z_v\\mapsto qz_v+a$, where $a\\in\\mathbb F_p$ and $q\\in Q_p^*$. Every single-label marginal of $\\mu_p$ and $\\mu_p^\\circ$ is uniform on $\\mathbb F_p$.*\n\n2.  *On the support of $\\mu_p^\\circ$, $$z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}\\in Q_p^*\\qquad(0\\le j<h).$$ The marginal of each ordered pair $(z_{v_j},z_{v_{j+1}})$ under $\\mu_p^\\circ$ is uniform on $\\{(x,y):y-x\\in Q_p^*\\}$.*\n\n\n3.  *Put $\\gamma=1/64$.     {
      "computational_model": null,
      "effectivity": "partially_effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $C<\\infty$ such that, for every integer $N\\ge1$ and every square-difference-free set $A\\subseteq[N]$, $$|A|\\le C N^{1-c}.$$ Equivalently, every subset of $[N]$ larger than $C N^{1-c}$ contains distinct elements whose absolute difference is the square of a positive integer.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.3"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "density-bound",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "|A|",
      "regime": {

        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
            "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $C<\\infty$ such that, for every integer $N\\ge1$ and every square-difference-free set $A\\subseteq[N]$, $$|A|\\le C N^{1-c}.$$ Equivalently, every subset of $[N]$ larger than $C N^{1-c}$ contains distinct elements whose absolute difference is the square of a positive integer.",
            "source_id": "PM-182.3"
          }
        ],
        "parameter_scope": "Fixed independently of the interval and the set.",
        "quantifiers": [

          }
        ]

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **lem-graph-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-count): Diagonalize the single-edge kernel by characters and telescope edge replacements. -> A graph constraint count with controlled discrepancy.
- **lem-list-mixing** [claim](#stmt-lem-list-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-list-mixing): Expand the centered kernel around a cycle and apply the graph count. -> An operator-norm mixing estimate.
- **prop-tuple-laws** [claim](#stmt-prop-tuple-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-mixing-2): Combine marked components, dominate unmarked errors, normalize, and isolate terminal components. -> The full law, its exceptional submeasure, and the nonexceptional law with the required marginals.
- **prop-signed-holder** [claim](#stmt-prop-signed-holder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-signed-holder): Use positive semidefinite cut forms and a finite sorting sequence of folds. -> Nonnegative diagonal forms and a signed mixed inequality.
- **lem-functional-properties** [claim](#stmt-lem-functional-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-functional-properties): Expand the diagonal integral, apply the signed mixed inequality, and average affine translates. -> A mean-dominating seminorm and coordinate-averaging contraction.
- **lem-conductor-decay** [claim](#stmt-lem-conductor-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conductor-decay): Tensor conditional operators over exact prime supports and use orthogonality of different supports. -> Decay for multilinear integrals with a high-conductor slot.
- **lem-lift-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-lift-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lift-moments): Bound conditional square sums, estimate square-function moments, and recover sums by random-sign symmetrization. -> Uniform fixed even-moment bounds.
- **lem-lift-specialization** [claim](#stmt-lem-lift-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lift-specialization): Keep the nonnegative contribution of a specified uniform fiber. -> Explicit fiber-size cost with remaining-conductor support preserved.
- **prop-square-kernel** [claim](#stmt-prop-square-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-square-kernel): Compare a signed actual-square kernel and a rational-frequency model on major and minor arcs, then apply the directional bilinear identity. -> A small quadratic-multiplier Fourier sum.
- **lem-exact-fiber** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-fiber) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-fiber): Sum the complete fixed-coordinate character group by orthogonality. -> Exact interval-fiber Fourier coefficients.
- **prop-scale-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-scale-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scale-transfer): Remove large conductors, split the fiber into square-step progressions, average square-affine lifts, and project to the shorter prime space. -> Uniform diagonal and mixed comparison with a shorter scale.
- **lem-good-residues** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-residues) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-residues): Construct local cycles of nonzero-square increments summing to zero and combine them by the Chinese remainder theorem. -> A positive proportion of good residue assignments.
- **prop-nonexceptional-contraction** [claim](#stmt-prop-nonexceptional-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nonexceptional-contraction): Average ordered interval choices, select an ascending cycle edge, truncate other slots, condition on their prime supports, and apply the actual-square kernel. -> Power decay on each good residue assignment.
- **prop-induction-recurrence** [claim](#stmt-prop-induction-recurrence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-induction-recurrence): Expand exceptional submeasures by inclusion–exclusion and split the exceptional subsets by their prime product. -> A strict weighted recurrence with a uniform ceiling error.
- **thm-functional-decay** [claim](#stmt-thm-functional-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-functional-decay): Choose the exponent after fixing the small modulus and the good-residue proportion; absorb errors and close strong induction. -> Absolute power decay of the functional.
- **thm-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-functional-decay-2): Use the constant Fourier coefficient, mean domination, and functional power decay. -> An absolute power saving in the cardinality bound.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-nonexceptional-contraction",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C$ and $N_0$ such that, whenever $N\\ge N_0$, $A\\subseteq[N]$ has no positive square difference, and $\\boldsymbol s$ is good, one has $$\\left|\\int\\prod_{v\\in V}\n  \\mathcal L_{[N],\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_{s_v}(z_v)\n  \\,d\\bigotimes_{p\\in\\mathcal P_N}(\\mu_p-\\varepsilon_p)\\right|\n \\le C N^{-\\sigma_0}.\n \\label{eq:nonexceptional-contraction}$$ The constants are uniform in $A$ and $\\boldsymbol s$.",

The constants $K,N_1$ do not depend on any proposed induction bound for $\\mathcal Y$.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: The local construction produces a closed cycle of admissible square increments at each factor of the fixed modulus.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Treat local residue increments as a construction of a large square-difference-free integer set. -> Admissible residue differences are not a certificate for avoidance of actual integer squares.
- **A2**: Extract a practical numerical density bound directly from the main theorem. -> The exponent and leading constant have no useful numerical value supplied by the argument.
- **A3**: Treat an indicator Fourier lift as a nonnegative probability density. -> The retained lift can take negative values.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The good-residue proof explicitly constructs finite local cycles of nonzero square increments; this implementation covers the local construction only.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON integer modulus m on stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: m is 8 or an odd prime at most 101; bool is excluded.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of 24 canonical nonzero square increments with sum zero modulo m; at m=8 all are 1.
# TIME: O(m log(m)) arithmetic operations, plus bounded primality testing.
# SPACE: O(m) residues and a fixed-length output.
# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpz modular arithmetic and exact primality test.
# FAILURE: invalid input or failed precondition raises ValueError and exits nonzero.
import sys, json
from flint import fmpz
value = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
if type(value) is not int:
    raise ValueError('integer required')
m = value
if not (m == 8 or (3 <= m <= 101 and m % 2 and fmpz(m).is_prime())):
    raise ValueError('unsupported modulus')
if m == 8:
    increments = [1] * 24
else:
    squares = {int(fmpz(x) ** 2 % m) for x in range(1, m)}
    if m - 1 in squares:
        increments = [1, m - 1] * 12
    else:
        a = next(x for x in sorted(squares) if (m - 1 - x) % m in squares)
        b = (m - 1 - a) % m
        increments = [a, b, 1] * 8
print(json.dumps(increments))
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **integer_scope** (hypothesis): Square-difference avoidance concerns differences in the integers; a residue-field constraint alone does not establish integer avoidance. Blocked use: ['Substituting modular square avoidance for the integer hypothesis.']
- **numerical_constants** (nonconstructive): The positive absolute exponent is extremely small, and the argument supplies neither a useful numerical value nor an optimal exponent. Blocked use: ['Claiming a useful numerical density threshold or an optimal exponent from the theorem.']
- **construction_size** (hypothesis): The finite construction is fixed independently of the interval and the set; changing its size changes the constants and exponent. Blocked use: ['Treating tuple size as a freely varying parameter while retaining the same constants.']
- **graph_hypotheses** (hypothesis): The graph-count estimate requires an odd prime, no loops, and at most one directed edge on each unordered pair. Blocked use: ['Applying the graph-count estimate to graphs with loops or opposite edges.']
- **list_partition** (hypothesis): The list-mixing kernel uses only constraints between the disjoint lists; constraints within either list need separate treatment. Blocked use: ['Absorbing internal list constraints into the bipartite mixing kernel without justification.']
- **prime_threshold** (hypothesis): Tuple laws and their conditional estimates apply only beyond the fixed absolute prime threshold. Blocked use: ['Using the tuple-law estimates at arbitrary small primes.']
- **signed_lifts** (counterexample_scope): An indicator input can have a negative Fourier lift. The truncated lift therefore cannot be treated as a probability density or a pointwise nonnegative indicator. Blocked use: ['Using pointwise positivity of an indicator Fourier lift.']
- **full_pair_law** (hypothesis): The exact strict-square pair description belongs to the normalized nonexceptional law; the full law has an additional exceptional contribution. Blocked use: ['Identifying the full pair law with the strict-square pair law alone.']
- **nonexceptional_positivity** (unknown): Reflection positivity of the normalized nonexceptional law is ABSENT; the contraction argument uses its conditional mixing and pair law. Blocked use: ['Transferring the signed seminorm inequality to the normalized nonexceptional law without a separate proof.']
- **moments_regime** (approximation): Uniform moment bounds require the stated interval length and complete exact-denominator support families, with moment order fixed. Blocked use: ['Applying the subpower bound to arbitrary individual frequency selections or varying moment orders.']
- **fiber_cost** (hypothesis): Specialization has an explicit fiber-size cost; it cannot be discarded from multilinear error estimates. Blocked use: ['Replacing specialized norms with unspecialized norms without the fiber factor.']
- **kernel_direction** (hypothesis): The square-kernel estimate requires ordered supports, strict square residue-class difference, and absence of actual positive square differences. Blocked use: ['Applying the directional kernel estimate to unordered supports or arbitrary residue differences.']
- **square_step** (hypothesis): The scale comparison preserves avoidance through a square progression step and square-affine symmetries. Blocked use: ['Using an arbitrary progression step as if it preserved square differences.']
- **good_residues** (hypothesis): The nonexceptional contraction is proved for good residue assignments; the universal scale comparison handles the other assignments. Blocked use: ['Applying the good-assignment cancellation to every residue tuple.']
- **coloring_scope** (edge_case): The coloring consequence requires only the endpoints to share a color; the square root of their difference has no color restriction. Blocked use: ['Claiming a monochromatic configuration including the square root of the difference.']

>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves a fixed power saving for sets with no nonzero square difference. There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $C$ such that every $A\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}$ satisfying $a-b\ne m^2$ for all $a,b\in A$ and integers $m\ge1$ has $|A|\le C N^{1-c}$. The bound is uniform for every integer $N\ge1$.

| `IsSquareDifferenceFree` | def |
| `power_saving` | theorem |

Comparator source `SquareDifference.lean` (469 bytes, sha256 `6cc7de3eec9a8283dd488c04c7867230a62cb029574a02979dfba3c92145f5fb`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace SquareDifference

def IsSquareDifferenceFree (A : Finset ℤ) : Prop :=
  ∀ a ∈ A, ∀ b ∈ A, ∀ m : ℕ, 1 ≤ m → a - b ≠ (m : ℤ) ^ 2

theorem power_saving :
    ∃ c C : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ ∀ N : ℕ, 1 ≤ N → ∀ A : Finset ℤ,
      A ⊆ Finset.Icc 1 (N : ℤ) → SquareDifference.IsSquareDifferenceFree A →
        (A.card : ℝ) ≤ C * (N : ℝ) ^ (1 - c) := by
  sorry

end SquareDifference

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that there are absolute constants <i>c</i> &gt; 0 and <i>C</i> &lt; ∞ such that every set $`A\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}`$ with no nonzero square difference satisfies $`|A|\le C N^{1-c}`$. This answers the fixed-power question posed by Green and Sawhney.
