 \label{eq:residue-average-functional}$$

<a id="eq:exceptional-signed-bound"></a>
For $B\subseteq\mathcal P_N$, define the nonnegative finite measure $$\kappa_B=
   \bigotimes_{p\in B}\varepsilon_p
   \otimes\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P_N\setminus B}\mu_p.$$ Its mass is $\eta_B$. The finite product expansion gives the following identity of signed measures: <a id="eq:signed-measure-inclusion-exclusion"></a>$$\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P_N}\mu_p
 -\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P_N}(\mu_p-\varepsilon_p)
 =\sum_{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N}
       (-1)^{|B|+1}\kappa_B.
 \label{eq:signed-measure-inclusion-exclusion}$$ For any real integrand $\Phi$, integration followed by the triangle inequality therefore yields $$\int\Phi\,d\bigotimes_p\mu_p
 \le
 \left|\int\Phi\,d\bigotimes_p(\mu_p-\varepsilon_p)\right|
 +\sum_{B\ne\varnothing}\left|\int\Phi\,d\kappa_B\right|.
 \label{eq:exceptional-signed-bound}$$ This step makes no sign assumption on $\Phi$. Notice also that the measure outside $B$ in each summand is the full measure $\mu_p$.

<a id="eq:exceptional-holder-moment"></a>
We first bound the summands with $q_B>N^\tau$. A summand of zero mass is zero, so assume $\eta_B>0$. Each normalized exceptional measure $\varepsilon_p/\eta_p$ has uniform single-label marginals: its invariance under simultaneous translation implies that any one label is uniformly distributed on $\mathbb F_p$. The same uniformity holds for $\mu_p$. Consequently every single-slot marginal of $\kappa_B/\eta_B$ is uniform on $X_{\mathcal P_N}$. No independence between different slots is needed. Ordinary Hölder’s inequality gives $$\left|\int\Phi_{\mathbf s}\,d\kappa_B\right|
 \le\eta_B\prod_{v\in V}\|G_{s_v}\|_{L^d(X_{\mathcal P_N})}
 \le C_\epsilon\eta_BN^\epsilon
 \qquad(\epsilon>0).
 \label{eq:exceptional-holder-moment}$$ The last inequality is Lemma <a href="#lem:lift-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lift-moments">15</a>, applied at the fixed even order $d$ and with the loss in each factor chosen sufficiently small. The bound $|f_s|\le M_0$ only introduces a fixed factor. The constant $C_\epsilon$ is independent of $B$, $A$, $N$, and $\mathbf s$.

<a id="eq:weighted-exceptional-mass"></a>
<a id="eq:large-exceptional-tail"></a>
The exceptional masses are summable with the extra factor $q_B$: $$\sum_{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N}q_B\eta_B
 =\prod_{p\in\mathcal P_N}(1+p\eta_p)-1
 \le\prod_{p\ge P}(1+p^{-3})-1\le\frac14.
 \label{eq:weighted-exceptional-mass}$$ It follows from <a href="#eq:exceptional-holder-moment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exceptional-holder-moment">[eq:exceptional-holder-moment]</a> that $$\sum_{\substack{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N\\q_B>N^\tau}}
   \left|\int\Phi_{\mathbf s}\,d\kappa_B\right|
 \le\frac{C_\epsilon}{4}N^{-\tau+\epsilon}.$$ Choose $\epsilon=\tau/2$. Since $\sigma_0=\gamma\tau/4<\tau/2$, we obtain $$\sum_{q_B>N^\tau}
   \left|\int\Phi_{\mathbf s}\,d\kappa_B\right|
 \le K_1N^{-\sigma_0},
 \label{eq:large-exceptional-tail}$$ uniformly in the residue assignment. Thus the number of exceptional subsets has already been accounted for by the convergent product <a href="#eq:weighted-exceptional-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weighted-exceptional-mass">[eq:weighted-exceptional-mass]</a>.

<a id="eq:small-exceptional-transfer"></a>
Now suppose $q_B\le N^\tau$ and $\eta_B>0$. Condition on all the labels at primes in $B$ under $\kappa_B/\eta_B$. The remaining prime law is exactly $\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P_N\setminus B}\mu_p$. For each realization, the function in slot $v$ is $G_{s_v}$ specialized at its prescribed labels in $B$. The mixed assertion of Proposition <a href="#prop:scale-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scale-transfer">20</a> applies to these arbitrary slotwise residues and labels. With $$D_B=M_0q_B,\qquad \lambda_B=\frac{D_B^2n_B}{N},$$ it gives $$\left|\int\Phi_{\mathbf s}\,d\kappa_B\right|
 \le\eta_B\left(\lambda_B^d\mathcal Y(n_B)+K_2N^{-\sigma_0}\right).
 \label{eq:small-exceptional-transfer}$$ The same inequality is trivially true when $\eta_B=0$. Crucially, the error and its lower threshold for $N$ are uniform in $B$ and in every conditioned tuple. No induction hypothesis has been used.

The ceiling in the smaller scale produces a uniform multiplicative error. For $q_B\le N^\tau$, $$1\le\lambda_B\le1+\frac{D_B^2}{N}
       \le1+M_0^2N^{2\tau-1},$$ and hence $\lambda_B^d\le1+u_N$. Moreover, $\sum_{B\ne\varnothing}\eta_B\le1/4$ by <a href="#eq:weighted-exceptional-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weighted-exceptional-mass">[eq:weighted-exceptional-mass]</a>. Summing the errors in <a href="#eq:small-exceptional-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-exceptional-transfer">[eq:small-exceptional-transfer]</a> is therefore harmless.

If $\mathbf s$ is good, Proposition <a href="#prop:nonexceptional-contraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:nonexceptional-contraction">22</a> bounds the first term on the right of <a href="#eq:exceptional-signed-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exceptional-signed-bound">[eq:exceptional-signed-bound]</a> by $K_3N^{-\sigma_0}$. Combining this with <a href="#eq:large-exceptional-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:large-exceptional-tail">[eq:large-exceptional-tail]</a> and <a href="#eq:small-exceptional-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-exceptional-transfer">[eq:small-exceptional-transfer]</a>, we obtain $$\int\Phi_{\mathbf s}\,d\bigotimes_p\mu_p
 \le K_4N^{-\sigma_0}
 +(1+u_N)
   \sum_{\substack{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N\\q_B\le N^\tau}}
      \eta_B\mathcal Y(n_B).$$ For a nongood $\mathbf s$, apply the mixed scale comparison directly with $B=\varnothing$: $$\left|\int\Phi_{\mathbf s}\,d\bigotimes_p\mu_p\right|
 \le(1+u_N)\mathcal Y(n_\varnothing)+K_2N^{-\sigma_0}.$$ Averaging these two estimates in <a href="#eq:residue-average-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:residue-average-functional">[eq:residue-average-functional]</a> gives <a href="#eq:functional-recurrence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:functional-recurrence">[eq:functional-recurrence]</a>, since the good assignments have probability $\rho$. The estimate is uniform in $A$, so we may take its maximum. Finally, $2\tau<1$ by the fixed parameter choice, and $M_0,d$ are fixed, so $u_N\to0$. All constants in the proof came from the preceding estimates and fixed parameters; none depended on an induction constant. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-thm-functional-decay"></a>
<div id="thm:functional-decay" class="theorem">

**Theorem 24**. *There are absolute constants $a_0>0$ and $C_0<\infty$ such that, for every positive integer $N$ and every square-difference-free set $A\subseteq[N]$, $$Y(N,A)\le C_0N^{-a_0}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-functional-decay"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-functional-decay</summary>

*Proof.* We make all remaining parameter choices before starting the induction. The finite construction fixes $h,t_0,\ell,d$; the conditional and kernel estimates fix $\gamma$ and $\beta$. The threshold $P$ was then chosen, and with it $M_0$, $\tau$, and $\sigma_0$. In particular the probability $\rho$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:good-residues" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:good-residues">21</a> is already a fixed positive number. We shall not enlarge $P$ after choosing the exponent below.

<a id="eq:absolute-exponent-choice"></a>
Put $r=1-3\rho/4$, so $1/4\le r<1$, and choose $$a_0=\min\left\{\frac14,\frac{\sigma_0}{2},
                  \frac{\log(1/r)}{4\log M_0}\right\}.
 \label{eq:absolute-exponent-choice}$$ Table <a href="#tab:parameter-roles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:parameter-roles">1</a> summarizes the roles of the parameters; among the listed quantities, only those in the last row vary with the interval length.

<div id="tab:parameter-roles">

| Parameters         | Role and order of choice                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
|:-------------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| $h,t_0,\ell,d$     | Fixed cycle length, marking depth, block count, and tuple size (Section <a href="#sec:tuple-laws" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tuple-laws">3</a>).                                                                                                     |
| $\gamma,\beta$     | Fixed conductor-decay and rational-frequency cutoff exponents (Sections <a href="#sec:functional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:functional">4</a> and <a href="#sec:square-kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:square-kernel">6</a>). |
| $P,M_0$            | Prime-law threshold and modulus containing all smaller primes; fixed before choosing the decay exponent (Section <a href="#sec:scale-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scale-transfer">7</a>).                                                    |
| $\tau,\sigma_0$    | Fixed partition exponent and common error exponent; chosen in <a href="#eq:scale-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scale-parameters">[eq:scale-parameters]</a>.                                                                                |
| $\rho,a_0$         | Fixed positive residue proportion and induction exponent; $a_0$ is chosen only after $M_0$ and $\rho$ in <a href="#eq:absolute-exponent-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:absolute-exponent-choice">[eq:absolute-exponent-choice]</a>.             |
| $Q,H,\mathcal P_N$ | Scale-dependent cutoffs and prime set, defined from $N$ in <a href="#eq:scale-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scale-parameters">[eq:scale-parameters]</a>.                                                                                   |

Parameter roles. Every construction size and exponent is fixed independently of $N$ and $A$; the Fourier cutoffs and available prime coordinates change with $N$.

</div>

Every quantity in <a href="#eq:absolute-exponent-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:absolute-exponent-choice">[eq:absolute-exponent-choice]</a> is fixed and positive. Thus $0<a_0<\min(1/2,\sigma_0)$, and <a id="eq:strict-induction-contraction"></a>$$\theta:=M_0^{2a_0}(1-\rho+\rho/4)
          =M_0^{2a_0}r\le\sqrt r<1.
 \label{eq:strict-induction-contraction}$$ There is no requirement that $a_0$ be numerically useful.

<a id="eq:induction-weighted-mass"></a>
For every finite $\mathcal P_N$, the bound $\eta_p\le p^{-4}$ and $2a_0\le1$ give $$\sum_{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N}\eta_Bq_B^{2a_0}
 =\prod_{p\in\mathcal P_N}(1+\eta_p p^{2a_0})-1
 \le\prod_{p\ge P}(1+p^{-3})-1\le\frac14.
 \label{eq:induction-weighted-mass}$$

<a id="eq:induction-threshold-choice"></a>
<a id="eq:finite-induction-base"></a>
Let $\kappa=(1-\theta)/2>0$. Choose an integer $N_*\ge N_1$ so large that, for every $N\ge N_*$, $$\theta u_N\le\kappa,\qquad
 K N^{a_0-\sigma_0}\le\kappa,
 \label{eq:induction-threshold-choice}$$ where $K,N_1$ are supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:induction-recurrence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:induction-recurrence">23</a>. This is possible because $u_N\to0$ and $a_0<\sigma_0$. The choice of $N_*$ is independent of $C_0$. Choose $C_0\ge1$ large enough that $$\mathcal Y(n)\le C_0 n^{-a_0}\qquad(1\le n<N_*).
 \label{eq:finite-induction-base}$$ Only finitely many values are involved, so such a finite constant exists. For an elementary bound, the lift in $Y(n,A)$ has at most $2n^{2\beta}$ frequencies and each coefficient has modulus at most $1$. Since its tuple law is a probability measure, $0\le Y(n,A)\le(2n^{2\beta})^d$. Thus, for example, $C_0=\max\{1,2^dN_*^{2\beta d+a_0}\}$ suffices in <a href="#eq:finite-induction-base" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-induction-base">[eq:finite-induction-base]</a>. This also covers $n=1$ and the cases of an empty prime set.

Suppose now that $N\ge N_*$ and $\mathcal Y(n)\le C_0n^{-a_0}$ for every positive integer $n<N$. Every smaller scale appearing in <a href="#eq:functional-recurrence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:functional-recurrence">[eq:functional-recurrence]</a> lies in this range. Indeed, $M_0q_B\ge8$ gives $$1\le n_B\le N/64+1<N\qquad(N\ge2).$$ The ceiling goes in the favorable direction for a negative exponent: $$n_B^{-a_0}
 \le N^{-a_0}M_0^{2a_0}q_B^{2a_0}.$$ Apply the induction hypothesis at every such scale in <a href="#eq:functional-recurrence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:functional-recurrence">[eq:functional-recurrence]</a>, and then use <a href="#eq:induction-weighted-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:induction-weighted-mass">[eq:induction-weighted-mass]</a>. Extending the positive subset sum to all nonempty $B$ can only increase the bound, and therefore $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal Y(N)
 &\le K N^{-\sigma_0}
 +(1+u_N)C_0N^{-a_0}M_0^{2a_0}
       \left(1-\rho+\rho
           \sum_{B\ne\varnothing}\eta_Bq_B^{2a_0}\right)\\
 &\le K N^{-\sigma_0}+(1+u_N)\theta C_0N^{-a_0}\\
 &\le \kappa N^{-a_0}+(1-\kappa)C_0N^{-a_0}
 \le C_0N^{-a_0}.
\end{aligned}$$ In the third line we used <a href="#eq:induction-threshold-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:induction-threshold-choice">[eq:induction-threshold-choice]</a> and $\theta+\kappa=1-\kappa$; in the last line we used $C_0\ge1$. This closes strong induction. In particular, increasing $C_0$ to cover the finite base range does not change the validity of the large-scale step. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-functional-decay-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-functional-decay</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Let $N\ge1$ and let $A\subseteq[N]$ have no positive integer-square difference. Put $G=\mathcal L_{[N],\mathcal P_N,Q}1_A$. Its constant Fourier coefficient is $|A|/N$, and every nonconstant character on $X_{\mathcal P_N}$ has uniform mean zero. The mean inequality in Lemma <a href="#lem:functional-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:functional-properties">12</a> and Theorem <a href="#thm:functional-decay" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:functional-decay">24</a> yield $$\frac{|A|}{N}=|\mathbb EG|
 \le Z_{\mathcal P_N}(G)^{1/d}
 =Y(N,A)^{1/d}
 \le C_0^{1/d}N^{-a_0/d}.$$ Thus $C=C_0^{1/d}<\infty$ and $c=a_0/d>0$ are absolute constants for which $|A|\le CN^{1-c}$ for every such $N$ and $A$. ◻

</details>

      "caption": "Principal proof inputs. The full tuple law supplies reflection positivity for the signed seminorm; the normalized nonexceptional law supplies the strict-square pair law for cancellation. Both have the conditional mixing used in conductor decay. The two lower branches are combined only in the weighted recurrence. Arrows indicate dependencies, not equivalences.",
      "figure_id": "fig:proof-map",
      "pdf_page": 5,
      "source_evidence": [

      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  box/.style={draw,rounded corners=2pt,align=center,font=\\small,\n              text width=3.55cm,minimum height=1.1cm,inner sep=5pt},\n  arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=5pt]},thick},x=1cm,y=1cm]\n\\node[box] (graph) at (-4.9,0) {Graph mixing\\\\Section~\\ref{sec:finite-field-graphs}};\n\\node[box] (tuple) at (0,0) {Tuple laws\\\\Section~\\ref{sec:tuple-laws}};\n\\node[box] (functional) at (4.9,0) {Signed seminorm and\\\\conductor decay\\\\Section~\\ref{sec:functional}};\n\\node[box] (moments) at (-3.5,-2) {Uniform lift moments\\\\Section~\\ref{sec:lift-moments}};\n\\node[box] (kernel) at (3.5,-2) {Actual-square kernel\\\\Section~\\ref{sec:square-kernel}};\n\\node[box] (transfer) at (-3.5,-4) {Square-step comparison\\\\Section~\\ref{sec:scale-transfer}};\n\\node[box] (cancel) at (3.5,-4) {Ordered-interval cancellation\\\\Section~\\ref{sec:ordered-contraction}};\n\\node[box,text width=6.4cm] (induct) at (0,-6) {Exceptional-measure expansion,\\\\weighted recurrence and induction\\\\Section~\\ref{sec:induction}};\n\\draw[arr] (graph) -- (tuple);\n\\draw[arr] (tuple) -- (functional);\n\\draw[arr] (moments) -- (transfer);\n\\draw[arr] (moments.south east) -- (cancel.north west);\n\\draw[arr] (kernel) -- (cancel);\n\\draw[arr] (functional.east) -- (7.1,0) -- (7.1,-4) -- (cancel.east);\n\\draw[arr] (functional.south west) -- (1.9,-.95) -- (-6.1,-.95) -- (-6.1,-4) -- (transfer.west);\n\\draw[arr] (tuple.south) -- (0,-1.25) -- (0,-3.2) -- (cancel.north west);\n\\draw[arr] (transfer) -- (induct);\n\\draw[arr] (cancel) -- (induct);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A dependency flowchart consisting of 8 rectangular boxes arranged across 4 vertical tiers:\n- Top tier (three boxes horizontally): \"Graph mixing / Section 2\" on the left, \"Tuple laws / Section 3\" in the center, and \"Signed seminorm and / conductor decay / Section 4\" on the right. A horizontal arrow points from Section 2 to Section 3, and another horizontal arrow points from Section 3 to Section 4.\n- Second tier (two boxes): \"Uniform lift moments / Section 5\" (left) and \"Actual-square kernel / Section 6\" (right).\n- Third tier (two boxes): \"Square-step / comparison / Section 7\" (left) and \"Ordered-interval / cancellation / Section 8\" (right).\n- Bottom tier (one wide centered box): \"Exceptional-measure expansion, / weighted recurrence and induction / Section 9\".\n- Routing of arrows:\n  - From the bottom of \"Graph mixing Section 2\", a line drops down along the left margin and turns right to point into the left side of \"Square-step comparison Section 7\".\n  - A vertical arrow points straight down from \"Uniform lift moments Section 5\" into the top of \"Square-step comparison Section 7\".\n  - From the bottom of \"Tuple laws Section 3\", a vertical line descends straight down through the center; it branches with a horizontal arrow pointing right into the left side of \"Ordered-interval cancellation Section 8\", and continues straight down with an arrowhead pointing into the top of \"Exceptional-measure expansion... Section 9\".\n  - A vertical arrow points straight down from \"Actual-square kernel Section 6\" into the top of \"Ordered-interval cancellation Section 8\".\n  - From the bottom of \"Signed seminorm and conductor decay Section 4\", a line drops down along the right margin and turns left to point into the right side of \"Ordered-interval cancellation Section 8\".\n  - A diagonal downward-right arrow points from the bottom of \"Square-step comparison Section 7\" into the top of \"Exceptional-measure expansion... Section 9\".\n  - A diagonal downward-left arrow points from the bottom of \"Ordered-interval cancellation Section 8\" into the top of \"Exceptional-measure expansion... Section 9\"."
    },
    {
      "caption": "$$\\underbrace{\\left((Y_{(u,w),i})_{i\\in[m_s]}\\right)_{w\\in\\Pi_+}} _{r\\text{ lists, each of length }m_s} \\quad\\longleftrightarrow\\quad \\underbrace{(Y'_{u,(w,i)})_{(w,i)\\in\\Pi_+\\times[m_s]}} _{\\text{one list of length }m_{s+1}=r m_s}$$ $$\\begin{gathered} j'_v=(w,i)\\ \\text{ uniform in }\\Pi_+\\times[m_s] \\quad\\xrightarrow{\\ \\text{restrict to }w=v_b\\ }\\quad Y'_{u,(v_b,i)}=Y_{(u,v_b),i},\\\\[2pt] \\mathbb P(w=v_b)=r^{-1},\\qquad \\mathbb P(\\text{all }v\\in V_+\\text{ select their own sublist})=r^{-n}. \\end{gathered}$$ The two steps when marking a block $b$. For a new address $u ∈ U_{S∪{b}}$, the old plus addresses above it are $(u,w)$, where $w ∈ Π_{+}$ is the word in block $b$; the prime on $Y′$ marks only a relabeling of the same entries. Regrouping the independent lists preserves their distribution without a loss. For each $v ∈ V_{+}$, with $u = u_{S∪{b}}(v)$, restricting its independent new index to $w = v_{b}$ recovers its old emitted label. This second step has the total probability $r^{−n}$.",
      "figure_id": "fig:list-regrouping",
      "pdf_page": 14,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:list-regrouping",
          "exact_quote": "The two steps when marking a block $b$. For a new address\n$u\\in U_{S\\cup\\{b\\}}$, the old plus addresses above it are $(u,w)$,\nwhere $w\\in\\Pi_+$ is the word in block $b$; the prime on $Y'$ marks\nonly a relabeling of the same entries. Regroup",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "A mathematical diagram displaying two sequential steps:\n- First row: A two-way correspondence $(\\longleftrightarrow)$ between a family of lists $((Y_{(u,w),i})_{i \\in [m_s]})_{w \\in \\Pi_+}$ with an underbrace labeled \"r lists, each of length $m_s$\" and a unified relabeled list $(Y'_{u,(w,i)})_{(w,i) \\in \\Pi_+ \\times [m_s]}$ with an underbrace labeled \"one list of length $m_{s+1}=rm_s$\".\n- Second row: A mapping starting from $j'_v = (w, i) \\text{ uniform in } \\Pi_+ \\times [m_s]$ leading via a horizontal arrow labeled \"restrict to $w = v_b$\" to the expression $Y'_{u,(v_b,i)} = Y_{(u,v_b),i}$.\n- Third row: Two probability statements: $\\mathbb{P}(w = v_b) = r^{-1}$ and $\\mathbb{P}(\\text{all } v \\in V_+ \\text{ select their own sublist}) = r^{-n}$."
    }
  ],
